Repeats within each route are byte-identical; changed output bytes must be assessed through the complete model and trajectory. All five measured contexts and 17 output buffers close. Both NPU controllers restore 300 MHz, with maximum sampled temperature 59.153°C.

An initial candidate used the older B2 layout and was stopped before NPU execution; its compile warning and the initial missing-ORT import failure are retained. Comparing against the currently selected layout prevents attributing an old layout improvement to FC2. The B1 K4 candidate now advances to complete aggregator-group validation; it has not changed the full-model measurement or passed the real-time goal. See [matched results, precision and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_frame_fc2_v104.json). No production backend, commit or GitHub push changed.

## 171. Frame FC2 K4 retains an 11.51% gain across the complete first aggregator group

The selected change now covers all five frame blocks in original aggregator pairs 0–4, including both complete B1 branches per frame block. The five global blocks, their existing FC2 K4 partitions, attention, inter-pair views and two public outputs remain unchanged. All 180 learned parameter values were checked against the original capture; reversing the static edit recovers the entire parent ONNX protobuf. The continuous ten-block board FP32 check passed: frame/global relative L2 was 1.1797e-6/1.6550e-6, with maximum absolute error 1.2457e-5/1.2159e-5. The fixed thresholds were not relaxed.

The normal flags0, core-mask1, bounded-1-GHz A→B→B→A comparison consumes the same actual saved NPU encoder prefix from v100. Six warm calls per route averaged **3.512596 s before and 3.108311 s after**, an **11.51% time reduction**. Each call runs all ten blocks continuously and returns both retained outputs. Input submission, execution, output conversion, owned copy, release and finite checks are timed; initialization, destruction and saved-output comparisons are separate. This is a repeated complete-group benchmark, not a fresh-image rate or full-model measurement.

Every baseline output is byte-identical to the corresponding saved v100 output; repeats within each route are byte-identical. Candidate-to-baseline FP16 relative L2 is **0.08912% for frame output and 0.12709% for global output**; both fail the strict elementwise comparison. These are NPU-to-NPU differences. The original CPU-input graph gate uses a different input and cannot establish original-FP32 error for this actual NPU prefix. Full-model pose/depth effects and trajectory quality remain to be measured. SDK internal/DMA fields fall from 96,392,512/764,813,312 bytes to 92,124,480/759,816,192 bytes, although the earlier isolated block had increased those fields. Memory allocation must therefore be checked at the integration boundary; these counters still do not establish physical 8 GB operation.

All four contexts and 32 output buffers were released after 16 calls. The NPU controller restored 300 MHz, with maximum sampled temperature 65.615°C. The board's nearly full data partition required streaming the 512 MB ONNX through a private ADB loopback connection into a sealed anonymous memory file. Legacy ADB lacked raw-shell support; the board Python also omitted Linux seal constants. The first gate stopped before inference and closed its memory file; stable Linux UAPI fallbacks plus an early kernel seal check resolved that compatibility issue without changing numerical checks. Both failed and successful receipts remain private.

This candidate is retained for full-model integration. The last measured complete original model remains **34.8033 s per repeated pair**; the new group timing does not replace it. Real-time pose/map cadence, native SL4 trajectory quality, 30-minute operation and the 8 GB target remain unmet or unvalidated. See [complete-group timing, numerical differences and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_group0_frame_fc2_v105.json). No production backend, commit or GitHub push changed; weight-bearing artifacts and captured arrays remain private.

## 172. Complete-model integration preserves the group gain but changes final geometry

The frame FC2 K4 candidate from v105 now executes inside the complete original two-image model on RK3588S. A new runner first validates all four original aggregator groups, then independently validates the candidate's static proof, board FP32 gate, compilation and normal ABBA receipts before replacing only group0. Its timed per-call loop is AST-identical to v100. Every image still traverses all 24 original DINO layers; all 48 aggregator blocks, four camera refinements, both DPT frames, retained concatenations, global20 clone/norm and original pose codec execute. Every stage consumes actual previous-stage outputs.

Under normal flags0, core mask1 and bounded 1 GHz, baseline first/repeat calls took **36.6252/34.9045 s**, and candidate calls **34.3660/34.5981 s**. The single repeat-to-repeat difference is **0.3064 s (0.88%)**. It is not a statistically established full-model speedup or fresh-image latency distribution. The integrated group0 call changes from 3.5133 to 3.1062 s; including its hash, context lifetime and original CPU consumer changes 4.4988 to 4.1224 s. The broader repeat aggregator path changes 21.2488 to 20.8684 s. The remainder still dominates total runtime.

All 21 baseline saved arrays match v100 byte-for-byte. Both routes repeat their five final outputs exactly, and each integrated group0 output matches its v105 same-input route. Independent inspection verified 70 saved array files and recomputed numerical comparisons. Against the same-image original CPU FP32 reference, baseline/candidate pose-encoding relative L2 is **0.63261%/0.61073%**, depth **1.63669%/1.63587%**, and confidence **6.34959%/6.40498%**. Strict elementwise comparisons still fail. Candidate-to-baseline pose-encoding relative L2 is only 0.03914%, but decoded two-frame relative rotation changes **0.0340 degrees**, translation direction **0.7190 degrees**, and translation norm **−1.07%**. Aggregate tensor error alone does not describe geometry changes; these pairwise differences are not trajectory ATE/RPE or calibrated metre errors.

Across both routes, all 40 contexts and 92 output buffers close after 72 SDK runs. Both controllers restore 300 MHz; maximum sampled temperatures are 65.615°C and 67.461°C. Peak process RSS is approximately 1123/1134 MiB, excluding the full physical deployment budget and tmpfs artifact residency. There is no 8 GB or 30-minute acceptance.

This establishes actual full-model integration of the candidate, with substantial performance work still required. The repeated candidate pair takes 34.60 s; it does not meet 20 Hz fresh pose updates, P95 ≤100 ms or a 2–5 s dense-map cadence. Native SL4 trajectory execution and quality acceptance are still outstanding. See [full-model calls, downstream numerical effects and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_full_model_fc2_v106.json). No production backend, commit or GitHub push changed. Raw outputs and weight-bearing artifacts remain private; repository and checkpoint licenses remain separate.

## 173. Original GPU attention: faster GEMM structure, still rejected for integration

The earlier GPU attention implementation spent 96.3% of its warm standalone boundary inside its kernel. v107 tests a distinct implementation: tiled QK GEMM, full stable row softmax in one score buffer, and tiled PV GEMM with explicit 4×4 register microtiles. It preserves the original captured FP32 `[1,16,2082,64]` operands, all keys and heads, the original scale, and the `[1,2082,1024]` consumer layout. No model, resolution, token or parameter substitution is involved. Compilation uses ordinary OpenCL 1.2, no relaxed math and no fused contraction.

Four board calls pass the unchanged original FP32 gate. Relative L2 is **6.9992e-7**, maximum absolute error **9.0599e-6**, and zero elements exceed `atol=1e-5 + rtol=1e-4 × abs(reference)`. All four saved outputs match one another exactly. The first call takes **647.1 ms**; three warm calls average **418.4 ms**, versus historical v103 r3 **796.4 ms**. These are separate short runs, not an interleaved comparison or a stream latency distribution. Warm kernel events average **185.9 ms QK**, **34.7 ms softmax**, and **164.7 ms PV**, totaling **385.2 ms**. The independent saved-array audit verifies outputs and receipts without executing the model.

This candidate is **not integrated**: even its kernel sum exceeds the prior approximately **245 ms** NPU attention profile. The NPU and GPU measurements have different precision, boundaries and clock policies; they support rejecting this implementation, not a matched backend speedup claim. Removing upload/readback alone would not close the gap. The new implementation also requires **297.10 MiB** of OpenCL buffers, including a **264.57 MiB** score buffer. The driver reports `CL_LOCAL_MEM_TYPE=CL_GLOBAL`; this identifies its reported allocation type, not measured DDR traffic or occupancy. The code's nominal 2.219 GB of score accesses is likewise not a hardware bandwidth measurement.

All 28 events, five buffers, three kernels, program, queue and context are released after four calls. GPU policy stays unchanged, NPU stays at 300 MHz, and maximum sampled temperature is **59.153°C**. No full-model improvement, trajectory quality, real-time or 8 GB acceptance follows from this component test. A distinct next hypothesis is explicit FP32 fused multiply-add in the two GEMM loops under the same precision gate; it remains unimplemented and unmeasured. See [measurements, rejection scope and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_gpu_gemm_attention_v107.json). No production backend, commit or GitHub push changed; raw arrays remain private.

## 174. Explicit FP32 FMA does not rescue the GPU attention candidate

v108 changes only the eight QK/PV accumulator expressions to explicit, correctly rounded FP32 `fma`; an inverse source check restores the complete v107 kernel. Every key, head, channel, tile, reduction sequence, tail and softmax expression remains unchanged. Four real board calls pass the original fixed gate: relative L2 **6.9989e-7**, maximum absolute error **8.8215e-6**, zero elements outside tolerance, and all four saved outputs byte-identical to one another. A separate saved-array audit recomputes these comparisons.

Three warm calls average **423.1 ms**, with **192.0 ms QK**, **34.6 ms softmax** and **163.7 ms PV**. The previous separate run averaged 418.4 ms; this short comparison establishes no useful gain, not a statistically established regression. All OpenCL objects are released, GPU policy is unchanged, NPU stays at 300 MHz, and peak sampled temperature is **58.230°C**. This result does not identify a hardware instruction or bandwidth bottleneck.

Do not integrate this implementation or expand this FMA candidate. Prioritize connecting fresh native518 predictions to the original submap/SL4 chain: fixed-image component and full-model calls still leave trajectory correctness untested. See [bounded FMA test and provenance](../results/original_native518_gpu_fma_attention_v108.json). No production backend, commit or GitHub push changed; all raw arrays remain private.

## 175. Fresh native518 observations now reach the original SL4 and map pipeline

The [native518 validation executor](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_slam/original_native518_executor.py) accepts the existing `ModelExecutor` prepared-pixel interface instead of calling the frozen diagnostic's fixed-image `OriginalPrefix.prepare()`. It preserves original normalization/token preparation, all 24 DINO layers per frame, all 48 aggregator blocks, four camera refinements, both DPT outputs and the original pose codec. Actual owned NPU outputs feed every subsequent stage. The adapter uses the frozen v100 baseline graphs, a single live SDK context at a time, and no feature or prediction cache. Its five owned canonical arrays remove only the outer batch dimension and map the codec's plural keys to the existing singular interface.

An actual RK3588S run processes TUM freiburg1_xyz pairs **[32,40] → [40,48]** through one unchanged Solver, `add_points`, SL4 optimization and map writer with `max_loops=0`. All five first-pair outputs match the complete v100 baseline byte-for-byte. The second pair shares the exact previous tail pixels and introduces a third distinct source image. This verifies input generalization and integration with the existing numerical baseline; it is not a matched original FP32 trajectory accuracy result.

The two image-read-to-pose-publication boundaries take **36.7352 s** and **34.3896 s**, including diagnostic prediction saves and first-pair parity checks. Prediction itself takes **36.6413/34.3094 s**; original point construction, scale estimation, factors and LM take **64.45/64.63 ms**. Final whole-map export takes **193.71 ms** separately. This is sequential integration, without paced arrivals or queue latency measurement. The approximately 34-second network cost remains the main obstacle to fresh real-time poses.

The completed chain contains **two submaps, four SL4 nodes/factors, three unique trajectory timestamps and 609,168 PLY vertices**. The original writer exports the map in the model's gauge. Its near-zero tree-factor residual (approximately 2.75e-27) demonstrates internal constraint consistency, not trajectory accuracy or metric map quality. The full map and model arrays remain in private evidence storage.

All **20 contexts, 36 SDK runs and 46 output buffers** are accounted for and released. The bounded controller completes in **83.68 s**, reaches a sampled maximum **64.692°C**, and restores the original 300 MHz NPU state. Peak process RSS is **1137.48 MiB**; model files, tmpfs residency and the full physical memory budget prevent treating this as 8 GB acceptance. The cleanup review also corrected a nested-head counter path and verified idempotent cleanup retry with a small synthetic owner, without host model execution.

This closes the fresh-input-to-native-SLAM integration gap. Longer matched trajectories, ATE/RPE, failure rate, map quality, loop support, 20 Hz fresh poses, P95 ≤100 ms, 2–5 s map cadence, 30-minute operation and physical 8 GB deployment remain unverified. See [integration evidence and scope](../results/original_native518_slam_integration_v109.json). The local validation sources are reviewable; no production backend default, commit or GitHub push changed.

## 176. Exact previous-frame encoding reuse and core mask 7 reduce complete native SLAM latency

The [native518 reuse executor](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_tail_reuse/README.md) retains one previous tail's owned DINO output before final normalization and pair-role tokens. Reuse requires matching model/configuration/core identity and exact prepared pixel bytes, not just a hash. Every new tail still traverses all 24 original DINO layers; all 48 aggregator blocks, four camera updates and both DPT frames execute again. Original role-specific tokens, pose codec and SL4 equations are unchanged. Both CPU patch preparations still execute. The cache retains 6,700,608 bytes, commits after successful cleanup and verification, and clears on failure or close. Ten generated checks cover ownership, changed pixels despite a matching supplied hash, key invalidation, fresh-tail execution and cleanup.

Two bounded RK3588S runs process the same real pairs [32,40] then [40,48]. With core mask 1, predictions take **34.8016/30.9910 s**; with mask 7, **29.8890/27.0809 s**. The second call in each route reuses the exact shared-frame encoding. Against the earlier same-input uncached v109 second pair at 34.3094 s, the fastest observation is **21.07% shorter**. Within this new comparison, mask 7 reduces the two matched prediction times by 14.12% and 12.62%. These are separate short runs, not an interleaved benchmark or latency distribution. An accepted core mask does not establish physical three-core utilization.

Each route's ten final prediction arrays, original SL4 homographies, TUM poses and 609,168-vertex PLY match uncached v109 byte-for-byte. An independent saved-data audit checks 33 NPY files and three source PNGs, resource ledgers and controller records. Prepared pixels and cache payloads were not separately saved; their identity is supported by the reviewed source and board receipts. Equality to the existing NPU deployment does not establish original CPU FP32 or ground-truth trajectory accuracy.

Each route releases all 20 contexts and 42 output buffers after 32 SDK runs. Maximum sampled temperatures are 64.692°C and 68.384°C; both controllers restore the exact original 300 MHz state. Peak process RSS is approximately 1155/1147 MiB on the 16 GB board, excluding the full physical deployment budget. Mask 7's second image-read-to-registered-pose publication takes **27.1631 s**, including diagnostic checks/saves and original backend work. Real-time, map cadence, trajectory quality, 30-minute stability and 8 GB acceptance remain outstanding.

The static audit reconciles the earlier full budget with current source: this cache-hit implementation still performs approximately **1.946 trillion DINO/aggregator MACs**, or **2.173 trillion MACs including the counted heads**, per new observation. These are conventional dense-product counts, not hardware latency bounds. The results establish useful original-model engineering progress, but no evidence-supported 20 Hz delivery route. See [measurements and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_tail_reuse_v110.json). Raw arrays and compiled models remain private, code and weight licenses remain separate, and no production default, commit or GitHub push changed.

## 177. Matched native518 trajectory exposes translation error and narrows the precision investigation

The [trajectory validation harness](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_trajectory/README.md) processes 17 real TUM freiburg1_xyz frames, indices `32 + 24*i`, as 16 overlapping original native518 pairs over 12.8 seconds of dataset time. The board uses the frozen v110 executor in four four-pair batches, with a cold encoder cache at each boundary. The comparison uses the complete original FP32 model on the authorized host CPU. Original PNGs and prepared pixel bytes match exactly. Both prediction sets then pass through one unchanged original SL4 Solver per route on the board, producing 16 submaps, 32 nodes/factors and 17 poses. These are offline captures and geometry replays, not a paced camera or latency test.

All 17 poses match the same ground-truth timestamps. Each route uses a positive Sim3 alignment; RPE uses adjacent selected frames approximately 0.8 seconds apart and the same fitted scale as ATE.

| RMSE | Original FP32 | Native NPU path | Change |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Translation ATE | 0.024309 m | 0.030243 m | +24.41% |
| Translation RPE | 0.016849 m | 0.023860 m | +41.61% |
| Rotation RPE | 1.411589 degrees | 1.274686 degrees | -9.70% |

Translation quality misses the intended 10–20% relative-error goal on this clip. The largest translation RPE increases from 0.02990 m to 0.05105 m. Independent saved-array review checks 178 NPY files, source identities, identical GT associations, original backend outputs and resource ledgers. Zero execution failures in these short runs does not establish a deployment tracking-failure rate.

Full confidence-mask IoU is 0.93047, with a worst frame occurrence of 0.59070. At 129,984 fixed pixel/occurrence correspondences, point disagreement is 0.06054 RMSE and 0.11373 P95 in the separately GT-fitted similarity gauges. This compares against the original FP32 map; it is not ground-truth surface accuracy or independent metric-scale validation. Depth-confidence tensor error alone is a poor guide to which output controls trajectory degradation.

A saved-output counterfactual, with no neural inference or backend equation changes, separates output groups. Supplying the original FP32 camera bundle (pose encoding, extrinsics and intrinsics) with native depth/confidence produces ATE/RPE translation **0.024147/0.016793 m**, close to the full FP32 baseline. Supplying only FP32 depth/confidence produces **0.032116/0.024736 m**; confidence alone also fails to recover the baseline. Both unmixed endpoints reproduce the prior original SL4 homographies and trajectory files byte-for-byte. These mixed outputs are diagnostic and cannot be deployed as a model. The result prioritizes camera outputs and their upstream inputs; it does not isolate CameraHead arithmetic from transformer error or justify changing the SL4 equations.

A separate same-pixel [32,40] control compares the current host FP32 forward with the prior board FP32 capture. Pose/depth/confidence relative L2 is approximately 5.96e-7/7.39e-7/2.59e-6; all three pass the saved strict `rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-5` check. This reduces concern about platform FP32 variation for that pair, without proving equivalence over the full trajectory.

All 160 contexts, 240 SDK runs and 320 output buffers are accounted for and released. Four bounded controllers restore 300 MHz and remain below 75 degrees C (maximum 72.076). Original-model inference still takes tens of seconds, and the short precision check establishes no real-time, 30-minute or 8 GB acceptance. See [full measurements and diagnostic limits](../results/original_native518_trajectory_quality_v111.json). Private evidence is retained; production defaults, commits and GitHub remain unchanged.

## 178. Independent head contexts shorten standalone attention, pending a complete-block test

The [head-parallel validation](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_head_parallel/README.md) partitions the original 16 global0 attention heads into contiguous groups of 6/5/5 on NPU masks 1/2/4. Every head retains all 2082 queries and keys, dimension 64 and the original scale, softmax and products. Export preserves the original attention node definitions; a board FP32 check passes before unquantized FP16 compilation. Genuine original post-RoPE Q/K/V captures supply both routes.

An interleaved ABBA trial compares the three independent contexts with one 16-head mask-7 context. Six warm calls per route average **392.704 ms** for the single context and **208.646 ms** for the partitioned route, a **46.87% shorter component boundary**. This includes input layout/copies, SDK input/run/output, owned output copies and release, joins, concatenation, projection-input reformat and finite checks. Context initialization/destruction and diagnostic hashes/saves are outside these call timings. All 16 calls produce byte-identical attention and projection-input outputs across routes and repetitions.

The first attempt rejected the actual logical NHWC `[1,2082,64,H]` input descriptor before any SDK run and closed cleanly. The revised adapter validates exact descriptor names, types and shapes, converts BHSD input to BSDH, and restores outputs according to their queried logical format. Actual outputs are NCHW BHSD. Requesting floating-point SDK outputs does not override their layout. Distinct-axis fixtures cover both conversions and rejection paths; the frozen base runtime remains unchanged.

An independent saved-array audit verifies all four stored payloads, 32 output records, source/reference identities, timer scopes and resource ledgers. Eight contexts, 32 SDK runs and 32 acquired output buffers are fully released. The bounded controller completes in 10.66 seconds, reaches 61 degrees C and restores the original 300 MHz state. Both routes retain relative L2 **0.00131118** and maximum absolute error **0.041678** against the original CPU FP32 reference; the unchanged strict precision gate still fails.

The standalone 393 ms baseline cannot be substituted for the earlier approximately 245 ms fused parent SDPA profiling row. Complete-block gains depend on new prefix/suffix I/O and context lifetimes, which this experiment does not measure. The next useful test preserves the original full global0 residual and MLP while timing these costs. No full-model speedup, trajectory acceptance or real-time result is established. See [component measurements and limits](../results/original_native518_head_parallel_v112.json). Raw arrays and artifacts remain private; no production default, commit or GitHub push changed.

## 179. Same-token CameraHead controls separate upstream propagation from head execution error

The [CameraHead diagnosis](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_camera_precision/README.md) uses the original native518 pair [32,40] and all 69 original camera parameters. The board first reruns the unchanged CPU FP32 CameraHead on saved original CPU tokens (p0), reproducing token normalization and all four activated poses byte-for-byte. It then runs the same head on saved actual NPU tokens (p1). The previous actual NPU head output on those identical NPU tokens supplies p2. No DINO or aggregator forward is repeated for this experiment.

The final pose-encoding difference norms are **0.015206** for p1−p0, **0.003034** for p2−p1 and **0.015396** for p2−p0. The vectors add but their norms do not; a negative dot product reveals slight cancellation. These raw norms mix translation, quaternion and FoV units. Upstream propagation is larger in translation and FoV, while the same-token head difference is larger in quaternion coefficients. The findings support inspecting upstream precision while retaining an independent head check.

Relative two-frame geometry uses the original saved camera-from-world matrices, `E1 @ inv(E0)`. Rotation differences are **0.239636 degrees** (p1 versus p0), **0.075935 degrees** (p2 versus p1), and **0.297580 degrees** (p2 versus p0). Corresponding translation-vector difference norms are **0.007159**, **0.001064**, and **0.006256** in the model's gauge, not metres. No GT alignment or SL4 scale adjustment is applied, and these pairwise differences are not trajectory ATE/RPE.

A further saved-only check reconstructs each retained frame/global concatenation. Camera-token relative L2 differences at original layers 4/11/17/23 are **2.435% / 3.279% / 13.772% / 7.227%**; patch-token differences are **7.292% / 9.060% / 15.260% / 12.969%**. These are cumulative discrepancies between complete original CPU and NPU paths at changing representations and reference norms. They do not identify error introduced by an individual block. The final camera-token bytes exactly match the inputs used by the head diagnosis.

Independent saved-data review verifies the head controls, same-token provenance, codec consistency and cleanup. The actual board run completes two head calls, eight refinements and eight codec calls in 13.00 seconds, under a CPU-only 180-second timeout. It changes no frequency policy and collects no temperature history. Component timings are not full-model performance. The single-pair diagnosis cannot definitively explain the 16-pair trajectory gap or establish a deployable precision fix. See [measurements and limits](../results/original_native518_camera_precision_v113.json). Original model execution and backend equations remain unchanged; raw evidence is private and GitHub has not been pushed.

## 180. Complete-block measurement rejects the three-context attention split

The [complete global0 experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_global_split/README.md) closes the integration gap left by the standalone attention result. It cuts the original block into a QKV prefix, the existing 6/5/5 attention partition, and the original projection/MLP suffix. The suffix receives the unchanged original input residual. All 53 nodes and 44 initializers, including all 18 learned parameter tensors, reconstruct the original parent protobuf exactly. The actual board FP32 composition is byte-identical to the parent graph and passes the unchanged original CPU reference gate (relative L2 4.33e-7; maximum absolute error 6.68e-6).

The actual NPU ABBA trial times complete fresh-context lifecycles for both routes. Each of eight calls includes constructor checks and hashes, SDK initialization, all input/output/layout copies, release, thread dispatch/join, destruction and residual/finite checks. The original full block uses one mask-7 context. The split route closes its weighted prefix before starting three independent attention contexts, then closes and joins them before opening the weighted suffix. Each route's first invocation is reported separately; the following three calls average **644.933 ms** for the original block and **1399.067 ms** for the split route. The candidate is **116.93% slower** and is rejected for integration.

Measured initialization plus destruction averages approximately **217.8 ms** versus **742.0 ms**. The original block's complete I/O boundary averages **416.8 ms**; the split prefix, head dispatch/join and suffix boundaries sum to **632.6 ms**, before its extra slice/concatenation and lifecycle costs. These observed scopes identify overhead in the implemented schedule. Their subtraction is not a measurement of a persistent-context implementation, and no internal operator profile establishes a particular compiler or hardware cause. Keeping contexts alive is therefore not an evidence-backed fix for this result.

All eight complete NPU outputs are byte-identical across routes and repetitions. Both retain relative L2 **0.0057064** and maximum absolute error **0.017195** against the original CPU block reference, failing the unchanged strict gate. The regression does not buy a numerical improvement. All 24 contexts, 24 SDK runs and 32 output buffers are accounted for and released; the controller exits successfully and restores the exact prior 300 MHz policy. Nine generated mixed-rank layout checks are separate from the real board trial.

Stop this fresh-context split schedule. The earlier 46.87% standalone attention reduction did not survive a complete-block boundary, and cannot justify expanding compilation across the model. See [complete measurements and evidence](../results/original_native518_global_split_v114.json). Original full-model real-time, trajectory, map cadence, stability and 8 GB goals remain unmet; production defaults and GitHub remain unchanged.

A bounded artifact review also leaves whole-block W8A8 unselected. Only one independently verified native518 global0 input is available in the examined records; the multi-pair FP32 captures saved consumer outputs, and historical 112-resolution calibration activations have a different shape and boundary. None of 23 examined native518 build receipts enables quantization. A matched native calibration/held-out set and actual compiler mapping would be new required evidence. Existing lower-resolution quantization results cannot establish native518 speed or precision, particularly while current trajectory quality already misses its target.

## 181. Calibrated native whole-block INT8 trades a small speed gain for large numerical error

The [native global0 W8A8 experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_global_w8a8/README.md) fills the calibration gap identified above. Four real TUM pairs calibrate and two disjoint pairs evaluate, with no PNG overlap against the prior trajectory and fixed-input checks. Every image traverses all 24 original NPU DINO layers, original normalization and role tokens, and standalone original frame0. The original CPU FP32 global0 supplies a reference on each exact captured input. This is a local operator screen: the standalone frame0 boundary has not been certified byte-identical to the internal deployed group boundary, and the data cover one scene.

The unchanged parent retains all 53 nodes, 44 initializers and 18 original FP32 parameter tensors before quantization. Both held-out FP32 ONNX checks pass the unchanged strict gate, with relative L2 **4.58e-7 / 4.51e-7** and maximum absolute error below **7.1e-6**. RKNN Toolkit2 2.3.2 then builds that graph using W8A8 `normal`/`channel` calibration on exactly four inputs. The resulting artifact is **16,054,672 bytes**, versus **29,798,710 bytes** for the FP16 baseline. Requesting W8A8 does not establish every operator's actual precision or placement.

An eight-call ABBA trial includes both held-out inputs in every phase. Every call creates and destroys its own context; construction, source/model checks, SDK conversion, execution, output copies and cleanup are inside the timer. After reporting the first invocation of each route separately, the following three calls average **623.813 ms FP16** and **516.679 ms W8A8**, a **17.17% reduction**. SDK execution within those calls averages **346.931 ms** and **328.071 ms**, only **5.44% lower**. Neither number is persistent-context throughput or a full-model measurement.

The numerical cost is unacceptable for integration. Whole-output relative L2 against original CPU increases from **0.6046% / 0.5961%** to **23.9313% / 22.9614%**. Comparing residual updates, `y - x`, increases the corresponding error from **1.1770% / 1.1719%** to **46.5912% / 45.1411%**. All repeated outputs are byte-identical, so this is a reproducible difference in the tested candidate. These tensor errors are not trajectory ATE/RPE, and no altered acceptance threshold or full-network quality claim is introduced. Reject this candidate and do not expand it across all aggregator blocks.

Floating-point SDK output is not a guarantee of floating-point model precision. The queried candidate output is signed affine INT8. All returned FP32 values lie exactly on its 256 dequantized levels. A saved-only nearest-level calculation gives **4.3141% / 4.2973%** relative L2 even for the closest possible representation of the original references on that output grid. Neither reference clips at its ends. The actual roughly 23% error is larger; this bound does not identify internal error sources or predict the result of changing only the boundary precision. `want_float=1` converts the representation but cannot recover lost information.

Independent saved-data audits verify captured inputs, references, candidate outputs, sample separation and resource ledgers. All eight benchmark contexts, calls and output buffers are released. The runtime controller completes in **8.647 s**, observes at most **61.923°C** and restores the exact prior 300 MHz policy. The separate successful capture controller also restores its policy and observes at most **73.923°C**. These short component runs establish no 30-minute or 8 GB acceptance.

Failed attempts remain part of the record. The first capture failed an inherited whole-`stat` checkpoint comparison after its upstream NPU calls; a later complete checksum matched the original weights. Access-time sensitivity is a suspected cause, but the failed run did not save both stat records, so its exact changed field is unknown. The replacement capture checks stable identity/content metadata, records access time separately and verifies the final complete checkpoint SHA. A checker source-table lookup and an interpreter missing ONNX were also corrected without loosening tensor gates; the successful check and compilation use the existing board SDK Python environment.

See [aggregate measurements and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_global_w8a8_v115.json). Raw tensors and weight-bearing artifacts remain private. The tested full-model path still needs approximately 26.7 seconds per cached pair and has not met the trajectory-quality target. Production defaults, commits and GitHub remain unchanged.

## 182. Original CPU Half control separates storage rounding from the larger NPU error

The [native DINO0 precision control](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_half_control/README.md) runs three original-class forwards on RK3588S using the same real `[1,1041,1024]` input and all 14 original parameter tensors. The untouched FP32 call reproduces all five observed points byte-for-byte. A second call rounds the original input and weights through Half storage, then executes the original FP32 operators. A third fresh instance executes with actual Half input, weights and observed tensors; it succeeds without a fallback. The original LayerNorm epsilon, attention, GELU, layers and token count are preserved.

Whole-output relative L2 against original FP32 is **0.05066%** for storage rounding alone and **0.07106%** for CPU Half, compared with the saved actual NPU result's **2.54823%**. Residual-update error, using the same original input in `y - x`, is **0.09003% / 0.12628%** for the two controls. This case demonstrates a much more accurate Half-tensor execution than the tested NPU artifact; it does not identify an individual faulty kernel, establish internal accumulation precision, or prove a universal FP16 error bound. Tensor errors also do not establish trajectory quality. The next precision investigation belongs in the original NPU execution path; switching models is not justified.

Three forwards complete in **20.06 s**, including setup, observations, hashes and saved arrays. Peak process RSS is **573.87 MiB** for this component test. These observed CPU times are not deployment benchmarks. Cleanup and source/input/parameter checks pass; the launcher makes no frequency writes and reads **300 MHz** before and after. It collects no temperature history. The board's calendar labels are months behind the host; retain those raw labels, use monotonic elapsed time, and do not infer valid UTC dates from them.

A separate source review rejects the original tracking head as a major compute shortcut: it still consumes all four retained aggregator levels and returns tracks, not the original registered camera poses. Exact reuse of the old image's 24-layer encoder output is already implemented. Role-token changes and full bidirectional attention prevent reusing later whole-layer results merely because one image repeats. No new substantial unchanged-work reduction is established. See [measurements and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_half_control_v116.json). Production defaults, full-model performance, commits and GitHub remain unchanged; real-time acceptance remains unmet.

## 183. Unchanged runtime dumps localize the attention discrepancy; optimization level zero produces the same binary

The [native DINO0 runtime diagnostic](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_attention_dump/README.md) captures 18 intermediate arrays from the existing compiled block without adding graph outputs or recompiling. The final tensor remains byte-identical to the untapped baseline. Independent saved-data checks prove that norm1 and head-major QKV match the prior compatible taps, that all three attention inputs reconstruct that exact QKV, and that the attention output reaches its flatten consumer unchanged. This avoids the attribution problem encountered when an added attention output changed compilation in the earlier experiment.

On those identical actual Q/K/V values, the fused NPU attention output differs from the original CPU FP32 attention by **4.54930% relative L2**, with maximum absolute error **0.22858**. Rounding the CPU output through Half storage leaves essentially the same difference, **4.54953%**. Thus upstream QKV differences and final output storage rounding alone do not explain this boundary's discrepancy. The comparison still crosses CPU FP32 and compiled FP16 execution: it does not identify a same-precision kernel bug, expose separate QK/softmax/PV values, or assign later cumulative norm2/MLP errors to those individual operators.

A separate board compilation changes only `optimization_level=3` to `0`, retaining the original validated ONNX, FP16 settings, parameters and all tokens. It produces the exact same **26,517,686-byte RKNN**, confirmed by SHA256 and board `cmp`. Stop this option here; benchmarking identical binaries would provide no evidence of an optimization benefit. No candidate inference or matched timing trial is claimed.

The diagnostic uses one context, one SDK call and one output buffer, all released. Its bounded 1 GHz controller observes at most **51.769°C** and restores the original 300 MHz policy. Debug dumping is not a normal performance measurement. Compilation takes **9.77 s**, changes no clock and has no temperature trace. Raw tensors, compiler intermediates, weight-bearing artifacts and the later unexecuted source revision remain private; the executed source and its minor memory-estimate erratum are preserved explicitly.

See [aggregate findings and evidence hashes](../results/original_native518_attention_dump_v117.json). These results locate a precision problem and rule out one ineffective compiler setting. They establish no complete-model speed gain or trajectory improvement; the measured approximately 26.7-second cached-pair path and unmet real-time target remain the applicable system result. Production defaults, commits and GitHub remain unchanged.

## 184. Native attention sharply reduces one precision error, but matrix execution is only a small part of its cost

The [same-QKV native attention experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_native_attention/README.md) first verifies original CPU SDPA directly on the exact NPU Q/K/V. FP32 reproduces the frozen counterfactual byte-for-byte; the same numerical inputs are exactly representable in Half. Original CPU Half SDPA gives **0.04444% relative L2** against that reference, versus the unchanged fused NPU output's **4.54930%**. This is a stronger control than rounding an output or rerunning a whole Half block that produces different QKV. It still does not equate internal CPU/NPU accumulation or identify a faulty suboperation.

A new board path uses native RKNN MatMul type1, FP16 operands and FP32 outputs, for every QK and PV product. Two persistent contexts process all 16 heads and all 1,041 queries/keys. The 15 storage-alignment columns are cropped before softmax; corresponding probability and V tails remain zero. Original scale and stable FP32 softmax run on the CPU, with an explicit Half probability boundary before PV. Relative L2 falls to **0.011224%**, but the original strict elementwise gate still fails: maximum absolute error is **0.0016365**, with **132,868** elements outside tolerance. No trajectory result or whole-model acceptance follows from this component improvement.

The initial four-call screen retains the first call and reports **517.480 ms** for the following three. All 32 matrix calls, dynamic K/V packing and uploads, synchronization, result copies, softmax, probability conversion and final output layout are timed. Context creation and destruction are separate and included in the summed lifecycle. The 32 bare SDK runs total only about **24 ms**. In the later direct-copy ABBA samples, QK output handling takes about **176 ms** and the crop/scale/softmax/probability stage about **266 ms**. These are composite stage timers, not isolated memory bandwidth or exponential-function measurements.

The controlled `ABBAABBA` trial changes only QK result copy order: direct strided access to the native buffer versus a contiguous owned copy followed by a transpose in ordinary CPU memory. Excluding the first call of each route gives **520.926 ms** and **516.783 ms**, only **0.7953%** apart across three samples per route. All twelve native outputs across both runs are byte-identical. This small difference does not justify expanding the copy-order branch. A shared-memory Mali softmax is the next candidate because it would target both measured costs; its new attention implementation has not been executed in this record.

Each NPU run closes two contexts and six buffers; the runs complete 128 and 256 matrix products. Both restoring 1 GHz controllers finish normally, observe maxima of **57.307°C / 58.230°C**, and restore the original 300 MHz policy. The separate CPU-only control reads 300 MHz before and after, without a temperature trace. An independent saved-data audit verifies fourteen output arrays, twenty-eight per-call comparisons, source identities, timing accounting and resource ledgers.

See [measurements and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_native_attention_v118.json). No original layers, weights, token resolution or pose equations were changed. The complete model remains at the earlier approximately 26.7-second cached-pair result; real-time, trajectory, map cadence, stability and 8 GB acceptance remain unmet. Production defaults, commits and GitHub remain unchanged.

## 185. Sharing native attention buffers removes a measured CPU boundary cost

The [shared-memory attention experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_shared_attention/README.md) executes native NPU QK and PV with an intervening Mali softmax. The GPU imports the actual QK FP32 C4 buffer and writes Half probabilities directly into the PV A8 buffer. Both imports persist across all heads and calls. No large score matrix crosses through a CPU staging array on this route. All 1,041 original queries and keys, all 16 heads and the original scale remain; the 15 storage-padding keys are excluded from softmax and explicitly written as zero. Standard FP32 exponential and reduction preserve the original formula, with changed floating-point reduction order and explicit Half probability storage.

An actual board `ABBAABBA` trial compares the existing native CPU-softmax path with the shared-memory path using the same two MatMul contexts and the same original QKV. Excluding each route's first call, complete attention averages **527.049 ms** and **161.919 ms**: an observed **3.255-fold speed ratio**, or **69.28% reduction**. The three GPU-route samples are **144.709, 142.651 and 198.396 ms**; this short experiment does not establish tail latency or sustained throughput. Each full timer includes all 32 matrix calls, dynamic K/V packing, transfers, synchronization, GPU status checks and event handling, and owned final output. Initialization takes **144.120 ms**, teardown **6.278 ms**, separately reported; the summed eight-call lifecycle is **2.914 s**.

The GPU kernel events sum to **42.655 ms** on average, but the full bridge including synchronization, submission, waiting and status handling takes **93.051 ms**. Native QK/PV SDK execution adds **28.793 ms**; other uploads, output handling and outer conversion/merge work remain. Kernel profiling is nested inside the bridge timer and cannot be added to it again or reported as the complete attention latency. This distinction makes the next integration decision meaningful: the improvement is over the native CPU-softmax component, not yet over the original fused complete block or the deployed model.

All four outputs within each route are byte-identical. The GPU route's relative L2 against original CPU FP32 on the identical actual QKV is **0.011224%**, almost unchanged from the native CPU-softmax route. Maximum absolute error remains **0.0016365** and the original strict elementwise gate still fails, with **132,881** elements outside tolerance. Separately, the saved final head's GPU probabilities are checked against CPU stable softmax on that exact NPU QK matrix, rounded once to Half. This diagnostic passes the unchanged tolerance, with relative L2 **1.1193e-7**, maximum absolute difference **1.90735e-6** and zero padding. It is a last-head diagnostic, not proof of strict FP32 equality for the whole block or trajectory quality.

The run completes **256 native products** and **64 GPU kernels**. All events, seven OpenCL objects, two duplicated DMA descriptors, two NPU contexts and six NPU buffers are released. GPU completion and import release precede NPU allocation destruction, including partial-initialization and queue-failure paths in the reviewed source. The bounded 1 GHz NPU controller completes in **6.062 s**, observes at most **57.307°C** and restores the prior 300 MHz policy. GPU policy is untouched; its frequency reads 300 MHz before and after, without a continuous GPU frequency trace. Peak process RSS is **359.98 MiB**, not a full-model or 8 GB claim.

One instrumentation defect is preserved explicitly: the bridge's initial metrics dictionary shallow-copies nested object counters, so its recorded `objects_released` values later reflect cleanup. Those nested fields are not an independent initial snapshot. Final counters and scalar live-resource counts provide the cleanup evidence; future reusable metrics must copy nested counters. The actual executed sources and receipts are retained unchanged.

See [measurements and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_shared_attention_v119.json). This result warrants complete original-block integration and a matched full-boundary comparison. It does not replace the approximately 26.7-second complete cached-pair measurement, establish improved ATE/RPE, or meet the real-time target. Production defaults, commits and GitHub remain unchanged.

## 186. A faster attention component does not make separate RKNN graph cuts a faster original block

The [complete DINO0 integration experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_shared_block/README.md) tests the preceding shared attention inside the real original computation. The exact original graph is cut into a three-node norm1/QKV prefix, the original twelve-node attention/layout reference, and a sixteen-node projection/residual/norm2/ErfGELU/MLP suffix. All 31 nodes, 23 initializers and 14 learned parameter values are verified; reassembling the original protobuf reproduces the parent exactly. The former complete graph with extra QKV outputs is not used as a prefix, which would repeat its attention and suffix work.

Before compiling either weighted cut, board CPU ONNX Runtime runs the original parent and the actual prefix→original middle→suffix sequence. The suffix receives the original input residual and actual middle output. The composed and parent outputs are **byte-identical**. All five reference comparisons pass both the unchanged original relative-L2/maximum-absolute gate and the separately recorded strict elementwise tolerance. Against original CPU PyTorch, the complete FP32 result has relative L2 **8.181e-7** and maximum error **1.669e-5**. Only the two weighted cuts are then compiled with the existing FP16, nonquantizing RKNN configuration; the middle reference is not compiled or substituted during the NPU/GPU test.

The actual board trial keeps the original fused context, both cut contexts and the two native MatMul contexts resident. An eight-call `ABBAABBA` comparison includes all normal SDK I/O, original shared attention, owned copies, layout conversion and finite checks in each complete block call. Excluding the first invocation of each route, the original fused block averages **175.830 ms**, while true prefix→shared attention→suffix averages **396.803 ms**, a **125.67% increase**. The candidate's component means are **60.999 ms prefix**, **180.693 ms attention**, **151.714 ms suffix** and **3.396 ms outer work**. The measured prefix and suffix alone sum to **212.713 ms**, already above the original complete block. The suffix input-set boundary costs **50.018 ms**. These measured boundaries identify why more attention-kernel tuning alone cannot establish a benefit for this decomposition.

The precision improvement survives composition only partially. Actual prefix QKV is byte-identical to the previous verified NPU QKV, and actual shared-attention output is byte-identical to the preceding component experiment. The complete block's relative L2 against the matched original CPU result improves from **2.54823% to 1.27336%**; maximum absolute error improves from **0.22929 to 0.10612**. Residual-update error, measured on `y - x`, improves from **4.52859% to 2.26295%**. Both routes still fail strict elementwise comparison. This does not isolate the remaining discrepancy to a particular suffix operator, or establish any trajectory-quality improvement.

All four repeated outputs within each route are byte-identical. Initialization takes **102.996 ms** for the baseline and **296.418 ms** for the candidate; combined teardown takes **79.342 ms**, separately reported. The complete eight-call summed lifecycle is **2.784 s**. Three weighted contexts execute twelve calls and release twelve output buffers; two native contexts execute 128 products and release six buffers; all 64 GPU events and seven OpenCL objects are released before their native storage is destroyed. Peak process RSS is **510.22 MiB**. The bounded 1 GHz controller completes in **6.633 s**, observes at most **55.461°C** and restores the prior 300 MHz policy. GPU policy is untouched. Static export, CPU checking and compilation have separate subprocess deadlines and no invented temperature measurements.

An independent saved-data audit verifies fourteen arrays, all reference comparisons, repeated boundaries, timer accounting and ownership ledgers. The reusable attention wrapper also deep-copies nested metrics, fixing the preceding experiment's initial-snapshot alias without altering its frozen GPU kernel. See [aggregate results and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_shared_block_v120.json).

Reject deployment or expansion of this cut-graph route. Preserve the useful original-operator and shared-memory evidence, then assess native-layout continuity across original weighted matrix products, normalization, residuals and ErfGELU as a separate bounded candidate. That candidate needs its own original-size full-block measurement; no gain is inferred here. The complete model remains at its earlier approximately 26.7-second cached-pair result. No default, commit or GitHub push is changed.

## 187. Continuous native buffers improve original-block precision, but the serial schedule remains slower

The [native DINO0 block experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_native_block/README.md) completes the proposed continuity test. Six persistent native RKNN type-1 contexts execute all four original weighted products and all 32 attention products. Mali imports 14 native buffers for original FP32 LayerNorm, bias, LayerScale, residuals, Erf GELU and stable softmax, writing native Half inputs directly. All 14 original parameter values are verified before storage conversion; both LayerNorm epsilons remain `1e-6`, and all 1,041 tokens and 16 heads execute. Static matrix uploads belong to initialization; dynamic head packing remains in every call. No reference intermediate replaces actual computation.

The complete eight-call `ABBAABBA` trial retains the first call separately and averages the following three per route. Original fused execution takes **177.689 ms**, versus **437.224 ms** for the native/Mali chain, a **2.461-fold duration**. Calls include transfers, all products, GPU synchronization/submission, owned output and finite checks. The candidate issues 52 GPU kernels and 270 synchronization calls per block. GPU device events sum to **66.448 ms**, nested within bridge waits. These timing boundaries establish neither a physical lower bound nor that every remaining millisecond is CPU or launch overhead.

Precision improves substantially on the identical original input. Whole-output relative L2 against original CPU FP32 falls from **2.54823% to 0.025077%**; maximum absolute error falls from **0.229286 to 0.00524735**. Candidate residual-update error on `y - x` is **0.044566%**. Both strict elementwise and fixed relative-L2/maximum-error gates still fail; 135,979 candidate output elements exceed the original tolerance. Repeated outputs within each route are byte-identical. Changing multiple original operators and floating-point boundaries together does not isolate individual error sources or establish trajectory improvement.

Initialization takes **100.228 / 381.337 ms**, combined teardown **68.759 ms**, separately from call means. Six native contexts, 18 buffers and 144 products close cleanly; 208 GPU events and all 38 OpenCL objects are released before native memory. The restoring 1 GHz controller completes in **6.627 s**, observes at most **55.461°C** and restores 300 MHz. GPU policy is untouched; its 300 MHz pre/post observations are not a continuous trace. Process peak RSS is **582.73 MiB**, without a full-model or 8 GB claim.

Reject deployment or model-wide expansion of the current serial schedule. Preserve the demonstrated original-formula and shared-buffer route as engineering evidence. See [aggregate results and source identities](../results/original_native518_native_block_v121.json). The complete cached-pair result remains approximately 26.7 seconds and its measured trajectory quality remains unacceptable; production defaults, commits and GitHub are unchanged. Raw research artifacts remain private.

## 188. Batching native GPU stages preserves output bytes, but still loses to the fused original block

The [batched original DINO0 experiment](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_batched_block/README.md) reduces GPU dispatches from 52 to seven per complete block by processing all 16 heads together for packing, softmax and merging. Four original weighted products and all 32 attention products remain. Thirty-six persistent type-1 contexts and 104 DMA imports preserve native A8/B16x32/C4 boundaries; all NPU products still run serially on core 1. All 14 original parameters and 1,041 tokens remain, without substituted reference intermediates.

R1's actual `ABBAABBA` trial averages **180.985 / 277.819 ms** for original fused/candidate execution, excluding each route's first call and averaging its following three. R2 processes four queries per 1,024-lane softmax workgroup while preserving each query's original 256-lane reduction tree. Three inactive tail slots read a clamped row and never write. R2 averages **179.143 / 284.153 ms**. Both candidates lose to their own matched baseline, by **53.50% / 58.62%**. R1/R2 are separate runs under an unchanged automatic GPU governor; no controlled causal layout gain or matched speedup against the earlier serial run is established.

Complete timers include upload, all products, GPU work and synchronization, owned output and finite checks; setup, teardown, comparison, saving and diagnostics are separate. Softmax GPU events average **56.580 / 65.968 ms**, nested within complete softmax stage walls of **86.192 / 94.327 ms**, including pre/post RKNN synchronization. FC2 stage walls remain approximately **59 ms**. These are measured boundaries, not hardware counters, physical lower bounds or proof that all remaining time is CPU or launch overhead.

All eight candidate outputs are byte-identical to the prior native candidate. Original CPU FP32 relative L2 remains **0.025077%**, maximum error **0.00524735**; both strict and fixed gates fail. Each saved-only audit verifies 16 arrays, numerical records, repetitions and ownership. Each run releases 36 native contexts, 108 buffers, 28 GPU events and 128 OpenCL objects after 144 products. Restoring 1 GHz NPU controllers observe **56.384 / 55.461°C** maxima and return to 300 MHz. GPU policy is unchanged and not continuously sampled; approximately **798 MiB** RSS is not physical 8 GB acceptance.

Reject deployment or full-model expansion of both schedules. The complete cached-pair mean remains **26.7345 s** and trajectory quality remains unacceptable; see [results and preserved evidence identities](../results/original_native518_batched_block_v122.json). Production defaults remain unchanged.

The proposed rolling two-context preload has now been executed in [§189](#189-rolling-group-preload-hides-initialization-but-depends-on-low-zone-memory). It targets existing hashing/initialization rather than changing original SDK operators; the following record separates initialization overlap, memory placement and complete prediction timing.

## 189. Rolling group preload hides initialization but depends on low-zone memory

The [rolling preload trial](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_rolling_preload/README.md) changes only scheduling of the four unchanged original aggregator groups. Ordinary flags0/core7 contexts retain all 48 blocks, parameters and tokens. Each dedicated worker owns initialization, run and close, with at most two reserved/initializing contexts; runs remain serial and use actual preceding-global output. The complete two-image route still executes both original 24-layer DINO chains, four CameraHead updates, both DPT frames and the original codec. Previous-image reuse is disabled for this matched full-model screen; no reference intermediate supplies a prediction.

R2 completes `ABBA` at **33.69636 / 27.22179 / 27.40540 / 29.62125 s** for model prediction from prepared pixels. PNG read/preprocess adds 34–45 ms in a separately recorded boundary. Hashes, initialization, normal IO, owned outputs and destruction remain within prediction; startup, saving and comparisons are outside. The recorded aggregator boundary averages **18.56878 / 16.13283 s** for A/B, about **2.436 s** less wall time. Serial aggregator SDK run sums remain **13.70681 / 13.84864 s**. Full means of 31.65880/27.31359 s differ descriptively by 13.725%, but first A has additional DINO/head overhead; this is not stable or purely preload-caused full-model acceleration. Overlapping worker durations cannot be added as uncovered time.

R1 had aborted with SIGBUS after one byte-matched A call when a 92,124,480-byte NPU allocation failed under low-zone pressure. Large total available memory did not satisfy that zone restriction. PFN inspection found **512,090,112 bytes** of group0 ONNX pages below 4 GiB, while the selected group1 compiled model was above 4 GiB. Moving the complete group0 export directory to disk and retaining an original-path directory symlink preserved both artifact hashes and all four compilation lineages; compiled models, drivers and memory policy were unchanged. The hash-verified relocation is reversible, and R2 completed afterward. This records placement-sensitive feasibility, without claiming a universal allocation guarantee from file size or total free RAM.

All 20 final arrays match the frozen native NPU pair byte for byte; the saved-only audit verifies 40 contexts, 72 runs, 92 released outputs and terminal workers. This preserves existing numerical behavior, including the unresolved v111 trajectory-quality failure. The 1 GHz controller observes **69.307°C** maximum and restores 300 MHz; **1,094.13 MiB** peak RSS and sampled zone/SDK fields do not establish physical 8 GB operation. Retain the experimental schedule and stop further preload microtuning without changing defaults. The **26.7345 s cached-pair** result remains a separate unchanged boundary. See [results and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_rolling_preload_v123.json); no timestamp-paced 20 Hz service, P95 acceptance, map cadence, SL4, 30-minute stability or trajectory improvement is demonstrated, and no physical impossibility claim follows.

## 190. Three native owners preserve the original block output but do not beat fused core7 execution

The [three-core original DINO0 trial](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_three_core_block/README.md) runs four weighted operations as twelve output-column partitions, preserving every complete K product and all 14 original parameter values. QKV partitions have 1,024 channels each, projection/FC2 use 352/352/320 and FC1 uses 1376/1376/1344. All 16 complete attention heads run in 6/5/5 groups. Fixed owner threads use masks 1/2/4; weighted shards share their leader's A and keep independent B/C storage. Mali reads twelve C4 outputs directly at their original logical channels, with no additional gather and unchanged bias, reduction, residual and activation order. This validates the native-size three-owner execution path, without assuming combined-core MatMul support or device utilization from an enum.

The fair original fused comparator uses **core mask 7**, validated by a separate byte-matched four-call preflight. In the actual `ABBAABBA` trial, excluding each route's first call and averaging its following three, A takes **151.464 ms** and B **293.912 ms**, a **94.05% duration increase**. Candidate complete calls include uploads, all 44 native products, GPU work/sync and finite owned output; setup, teardown, comparison and diagnostics are separate. Candidate complete softmax, FC2, QK and PV stages average **90.925 / 33.489 / 14.427 / 17.234 ms**. Softmax's **56.577 ms** GPU event duration is nested inside its stage wall. Host intervals establish overlapping blocking SDK calls, not physical core occupancy or an isolated kernel/launch cause. Cross-run comparisons with older core1 baselines do not define this trial's gain.

All four B outputs are byte-identical to the preceding native candidate. Whole-output relative L2 against original CPU FP32 remains **0.025077%**, maximum error **0.00524735**; both strict and fixed gates still fail, as independently recomputed for 16 saved arrays. All 44 native contexts, 132 buffers, 176 runs, 460 bindings and 1,020 syncs are accounted for; 28 GPU events and 136 OpenCL objects release before native memory. Borrower contexts close before shared-A leaders, and all three workers terminate. The restoring NPU controller completes in **7.602 s**, observes **55.461°C** maximum and returns to 300 MHz; GPU policy is unchanged. **862.695 MiB** peak RSS is a process observation, not physical 8 GB acceptance.

Reject deployment and stop further microtuning of this native three-core schedule. Preserve the useful supported-core, original-output and ownership evidence in [results and source identities](../results/original_native518_three_core_block_v124.json). The complete **26.7345 s cached-pair** measurement, failed v111 trajectory assessment and separate R123 cold-pair preload screen remain unchanged. No production default changes, whole-model speedup, trajectory improvement or executable 20 Hz solution follows from this component trial.


## 191. A graph-wide residual basis is a numerical representation hypothesis, not a measured speedup

The [graph review](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_graph_basis/README.md) changes the unit of investigation from individual kernels to the original transformer chain. Existing execution already keeps 10/14/12/12 aggregator blocks inside four NPU graphs. A separate core1 group0 profile assigns 52.23% of its operator time to SDPA and 2.05% to explicit layout/bridge rows; these are not full48 ordinary-call fractions. Native query-only tiling with every original key was already tested in R99 and made a complete block slower, as did the later mixed-device schedules. Those routes are not new candidates.

The selected new hypothesis uses a fixed mean-preserving orthogonal basis throughout the 1024-channel residual stream. With `R=((J4-2I4)/2)` raised to its fifth tensor power, `R.T R=I` and `R ones=ones`; original bare LayerNorm therefore commutes with the basis in real arithmetic, with unchanged epsilon. Original norm affine terms fold into QKV/FC1, while original output projection/FC2, their biases and LayerScale fold into the output basis. QKV and FC1 results retain their original coordinates, so original q/k normalization, RoPE, softmax and Erf remain untouched. This derives transformed constants from the original checkpoint without learning a new model. All original layers, tokens and dense products remain.

Exact integer base checks and generated scalar arithmetic validate the fold orientation and LayerNorm condition up to dimension 1024, with maximum float64 discrepancy about 1.1e-14. An arbitrary orthogonal matrix fails the generated LayerNorm counterexample. This is mathematical scaffolding, not original-weight execution, calibration or a hardware result. The potential benefit is improved low-precision channel representation; there is no established MAC reduction, SDPA acceleration or quantized accuracy gain. Prior compiler LayerScale fusion alone is not counted as a new contribution.

Keep the basis across compiled groups and restore canonical retained features at the original head boundaries unless a separate consumer transformation is proven. CameraHead's input-dependent adaptive channel gates prevent blindly absorbing the inverse into a static weight. The next decisive gate is the complete original FP32 frame/global pair on identical real inputs, followed by actual compiler placement and complete multi-block timing before a full-model or trajectory decision. See [equations, source identities and limits](../results/original_native518_graph_basis_v125.json). This review changes no production defaults, runs no VGGT inference and makes no real-time acceptance claim.

## 192. A persistent residual basis passes the first original frame/global FP32 gate

The [complete frame0/global0 gate](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_basis_pair/README.md) moves the residual coordinate system across two original blocks, rather than inserting a conversion around every operator. A preserves the original graph, F controls affine/LayerScale folding with identity basis, and B uses the mean-preserving 1024-channel basis from §191. All 36 original learned tensors are verified before deriving constants. B feeds its actual rotated frame result into global0 and restores only the canonical endpoints. Original 1041 tokens per frame, all 2082 global keys, q/k normalization, RoPE, Erf and FC2K4 slice/sum order remain.

The recovered R2 board CPU run completes all six graph calls. Every endpoint passes the unchanged relative L2 `<=1e-5`/maximum error `<=1e-3` gate and independent elementwise diagnostics. Across two inputs, B versus A has worst relative L2 **8.6705e-7** and maximum error **7.6294e-6**. B versus matched original Torch has **1.3610e-6 / 1.6332e-5**. The independent saved-only audit verifies all 12 outputs; the low-order values differ from A. Both inputs come from the **same TUM pair [32,40]**, with original CPU versus actual NPU prefix provenance. Only the CPU-prefix case has matched Torch endpoints; these are not independent scenes or held-out quality data.

Rootfs export/check controller totals are **6.5208 / 37.3958 s**, with maximum **59.153 / 70.230°C**. Checking includes four cooling pauses; NPU frequency is only observed at the post-reboot 1 GHz value, with no clock writes. These CPU diagnostic times establish no NPU performance. The three graphs and 12 endpoints total **413,717,148 B**, within the 500 MiB evidence budget. Derived weights replace obsolete large payloads within each graph; the three comparison graphs each still contain their own weights.

Host/board restarts of unestablished cause erased the original tmpfs graphs and arrays before archival. Source/config recovery matches their original SHA256 pins, the three regenerated graph hashes match the first observed run, and the R2 outputs now form the authoritative saved evidence. Preserve controls/logs without synthesizing lost receipts or arrays; use persistent evidence storage. No NPU build, low-precision gain, all48/head integration or trajectory improvement follows yet. All original learned dense products remain; B adds diagnostic basis conversions. Production defaults remain unchanged, and the 20 Hz/P95 goals remain unmet. See [aggregate identities and limits](../results/original_native518_basis_pair_v126.json).


## 193. The continuous basis has no measured FP16 speed or precision benefit

The [complete original frame/global FP16 screen](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_basis_pair_fp16/README.md) compiles the three FP32-gated graphs with the same nonquantizing RKNN configuration. Six sequential contexts run `A F B B F A`, each on the original CPU-prefix and actual NPU-prefix arrays of one real TUM pair. Every call includes input preparation, SDK IO, both complete blocks, canonical output restoration, owned copies, release and finite checks. Initialization/destruction are measured separately; comparisons and artifact saving are excluded from those timers.

Complete-call means are **852.748 / 849.710 / 957.398 ms** for original A, folding-only F and persistent-basis B. F differs by only **0.36%**; B is **12.27% slower** than A. Four calls per variant are a component screen, not stable throughput or P95. Global endpoint relative L2 against the matched original A FP32 is approximately **0.00615–0.00636** for all three; there is no material FP16 accuracy improvement. The independent saved-array audit verifies 24 outputs, 12 references, two inputs and 72 recorded comparisons. Original local-global residual-update error is also reported, without a denominator floor. Every repeat is byte-identical.

All six contexts and 24 output buffers close cleanly after 12 runs. The restoring 1 GHz controller completes in **18.919 s**, observes **66.538°C** maximum and restores the actual post-reboot **1 GHz** baseline, governor and limits. The worker's **421.34 MiB** peak RSS is not an 8 GB deployment assessment. Compilation takes **144.18 / 153.63 / 178.06 s** under separate CPU-only thermal controllers; these are not inference times. Graphs, compiled models and outputs are privately archived on persistent storage. No default, checkpoint, driver or GPU policy changes, and no GitHub push.

Close this FP16 candidate without claiming acceleration. The fixed basis remains a hypothesis for internal quantization conditioning, requiring fresh, disjoint calibration and held-out frame0 inputs and equal precision policies for A/F/B. Prior R115 output-grid error is a lower bound, not proof that output quantization caused most internal error. `want_float=1` alone cannot restore lost precision.

The graph-wide review also distinguishes removable diagnostic boundaries from necessary computation. R127 adds dense input and two output basis conversions; these must not be extrapolated as a per-layer cost. DPT's input LayerNorm affine and first projection can algebraically absorb the inverse basis. CameraHead's dynamic gates prevent that fold, but only camera-token rows need restoration. DINO's final affine LayerNorm followed by aggregator normalization remains another noncommuting boundary. These are source/algebra findings, not implemented deployment gains. A separate candidate streams complete FC1→original GELU→matching FC2 channel slices in one graph; it must prove placement and reduced live intermediate storage before integration. No full-model timing, trajectory quality or real-time target has improved in this screen. See [results and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_basis_pair_fp16_v127.json).

## 194. Mixed precision needs actual boundary and numerical evidence

The [native basis quantization screen](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_basis_quantization/README.md) collects six source-disjoint frame0 inputs after complete original NPU DINO execution. Four inputs calibrate A/F/B and two are held out; all 18 same-input two-block CPU calls pass the original FP32 numeric gate, with all 36 saved outputs independently verified. This proves conditional frame0/global0 equivalence, not a full Torch prediction or trajectory result. All three manual mixed builds complete after preserved clean deadline/dependency failures. Offline calibration peaks at **12,323–12,397 MiB** RSS on a 16 GB board, which is not inference memory or 8 GB calibration acceptance.

Actual placement differs from selector intent: A/F share the INT8 canonical frame output with global reshape, while B has a separate FP16 boundary and CPU transpose. Six FP16 residual Adds do not protect the whole residual stream. The calibration-only normal/profile diagnostic reproduces bytes, including A/F's invalid global values, and balances ten contexts/twenty released outputs. B is finite but has **20.7558%** global relative L2 against original same-input A FP32, versus **0.6073%** for original A FP16. An unchanged A dump reproduces both final outputs; independent audit verifies 102 tensors and first observes nonfinites at global-input Reshape057 after the finite frame boundary. This does not prove a specific bitcast, alias cause or SDK bug. A `remove_reshape=True` discriminator is rejected for inconsistency with step1 and yields no new model or runtime.

A further unchanged-B policy retains full attention FP16 and eligible MLP INT8 using the same calibration. All four new outputs are finite and flags0/8 byte-identical; global relative L2 falls to **8.6424%**, but both original-A and own-B FP32 numeric/strict comparisons still fail. Reject this candidate without a basis-cure or sole-error-source claim. Actual profiling matches 84 compiled rows: 63 FP16/NPU, 17 INT8/NPU and four FP16/CPU. Three FP16 SDPA rows occupy **391.604 ms / 52.03%** of diagnostic operator time; this is not normal-call latency or a causal speedup comparison. The canonical frame-output CPU transpose takes **118.928 ms / 15.8%** of diagnostic operator time, motivating consumer-layout review without a predicted removable gain. Two contexts/two calls/four buffers close; the controller observes **58.230°C** and restores its prior 1 GHz policy. Mixed-precision runtime heldouts and complete-model acceptance remain untested. See [results and evidence identities](../results/original_native518_basis_quantization_v128.json).

## 195. Joint MLP source order does not establish a lower-memory NPU schedule

The [original frame0/global0 joint-MLP screen](../deploy/rk3588/validation/original_native518_joint_mlp/README.md) splits global FC1 and the original Erf GELU into four matching 1024-channel branches feeding the existing FC2 K4 products and left-associated sums. All 4096 hidden neurons, native tokens and 36 original parameter tensors remain. Six genuine pre-frame0 inputs yield 12 complete FP32 outputs byte-identical to original A. This proves the conditional two-block transformation, without claiming full-model accuracy. Its unsplit frame FC2 matches the current R110/v100 baseline; the previously tested R104–R106 frame-FC2 K4 candidate is not incorporated.

The source interleaves each FC1/GELU branch with its FC2 consumer, but the actual compiler lists all four FC1/ConvExGelu rows before any FC2. The intended reduced intermediate lifetime is therefore unestablished; compiler listings are not device timelines. Normal core1/flags0 A/J/J/A calls on two heldouts average **853.103 ms for A and 843.925 ms for J**. The **1.0758% descriptive difference fails the predeclared 10% acceleration screen**. All 16 saved NPU outputs are byte-identical across A/J and repeats, while both routes retain the same failing fixed/strict FP32 comparisons. SDK-reported internal allocation remains **92,124,480 bytes** in both routes; it does not demonstrate physical memory savings.

Reject the current joint schedule for deployment. Four contexts, eight runs and 16 buffers close cleanly; the restoring controller observes **63.769°C** maximum. Complete owned SDK IO is timed, with context lifecycle and saved-array comparisons separate. No default, complete-model timing, trajectory quality, P95 or 8 GB acceptance changes. Preserve the source-order/compiler-order distinction when designing a future connected graph; neither source interleaving nor an artificial zero-valued edge guarantees vendor scheduling. See [verified results and audit identities](../results/original_native518_joint_mlp_v129.json).

No NPU compile, mixed-precision consumer gain, activated-depth/map quality, trajectory result, realtime/P95 or 8 GB acceptance follows.
