## 197. A legacy ADB shell can hide remote command failure

After reconnecting the board, an explicit remote failing command reported its status as `1` while the host ADB process returned `0`. Therefore `subprocess.run(..., check=True)` around this legacy `adb shell` does not alone establish that a remote preflight succeeded. The current K-centering and V-fold transports now require explicit `EXISTS`/`MISSING` output for fresh remote paths. They additionally verify artifact hashes and terminal worker/controller receipts; a host transport return code is not a numerical or cleanup verdict. This observation concerns the connected legacy service, not every ADB version.

The restart cleared temporary experiment files and left the existing work partition unmounted. The partition was identified read-only before restoring its previous writable mount. Only already archived experiment prerequisites were copied back, with host and board SHA256 verification. No calibration was repeated to reconstruct records, and existing failed transport evidence was retained.

## 198. A public intermediate output can change the precision of an internal residual

The [frame-to-global boundary experiment](../results/original_native518_global_boundary_v134.json) distinguishes three observed outcomes. The original A20 mixed graph has a finite frame output followed by a nonfinite global output on calibration pair0. Removing only two FLOAT16 bridge requests causes an explicit INT8-to-FLOAT16 conversion before the global residual Add and makes the global output finite. Its global relative L2 against original A FP32 remains 21.5217%, compared with 0.607276% for the matched original FP16 baseline. This does not prove an SDK bitcast or aliasing root cause.

Removing only the public frame-output declaration preserves all 143 original computation nodes and 79 initializers, with four board FP32 global outputs byte-identical to their original references. Fresh calibration preserves all 142 shared quantization-catalogue entries. The actual compiled graph now keeps both frame outputs, Concat, Reshape and the global residual input in FLOAT16, while a separate conversion supplies the INT8 normalization branch. All six residual Adds remain FLOAT16 and all three SDPA operators remain INT8. A public floating output or a requested selector alone would not have established this internal path.

The single-output NPU graph is finite, but global relative L2 is still 21.0486%: only a 2.20% relative error reduction and 34.66 times the matched FP16 error. Preserve the working diagnostic representation; do not deploy it as an accuracy fix. Normal call boundaries are 753.55/707.62 ms for the two-output control/single-output graph, one call each; output counts differ and these are not stable throughput measurements. No original head feature tap is authorized for removal, and no heldout, trajectory, 20 Hz/P95, long-run or 8 GB acceptance follows. Worker outputs, compiler evidence and restored controller states remain privately archived; GitHub was not pushed.

## 199. Verify observation parity before attributing an internal error

The [attention error localization](../results/original_native518_attention_error_localization_v135.json) uses two separate observation methods. The NPU uses the unchanged compiled model with process-local runtime dumping and verifies its sole output against the normal call. All 97 saved NPU tensors are finite. The internal final layout and both residual Adds are checked against actual values; the Adds exactly match float32 summation followed by FP16 rounding. These checks support the observed tensor correspondence without claiming that final parity alone proves every hidden internal operation unchanged.

The dominant error is already in the global attention update: relative L2 79.4606%, with 98.57% of its squared error in the cross-token mean. Frame-skip, attention-update and MLP-update error norms are 7.209, 46.997 and 5.687; the MLP partly offsets the large attention mean error. A signed additive decomposition closes within `2.22e-16`; its projection coefficients are accounting terms, not independent error-energy or causal percentages. Projection, LayerScale, upstream propagation and fused attention remain unresolved within this update. No probability matrix was observed, so probability-row-mass loss remains a hypothesis.

This narrows the next graph experiment to moving the value mean outside the weighted sum, with an explicit original-FP32 equivalence gate before calibration. It does not justify empirical output-bias correction, replacing the model, or a performance claim. The CPU observer and one NPU diagnostic released their resources; the CPU changed no clocks and the authorized NPU controller restored its prior policy. Raw artifacts stay private and no GitHub push was made.

A new mapper resolves actual weighted QK/V producers and requests FLOAT16 only downstream of V. Blindly applying the old shared-Split selector would promote the weighted V leaf. Complete-call means are **695.811/696.745 ms** on pair4 and **689.191/680.837 ms** on pair5 for parent/split; these small mixed changes establish no material speed gain. Lifecycle, saves and comparisons remain outside timing. Two contexts, eight runs and eight buffers close, and the restoring 1 GHz controller returns to its exact prior policy.

Close the pure QKV-split route without a deployment gain. The comparison covers complete precision configurations: the parent's floating shared Split and the candidate's independent INT8 V-product boundary differ, so it cannot isolate one quantization mechanism. ## 207. Commute the dynamic value mean across an original affine before measuring new grids

The [R142 record](../results/original_native518_value_affine_commutation_v142.json) contains two separate graph candidates derived from the existing original QKV-split/V-mean parent. Both retain all 2,082 keys, 16 heads, original layers and full dense products. All **81 parent initializer protos stay byte-identical**; each adds only a shape initializer and has a whole-parent inverse. Their real-arithmetic identities hold for arbitrary attention row mass relative to that existing centered representation. They establish no new property of fused quantized softmax or of the earlier centering identity against uncentered attention.

The **input-side** candidate moves the mean through the unchanged original V affine: `mu_V = mean(X) W_V + b_V`, while the full centered product becomes `(X - mean(X)) W_V`. The original bias occurs once on the mean branch; the original QK, softmax, PV, restoration and output projection remain. The full **2,183,135,232 V MACs** remain, with an additional **1,048,576 one-row MACs**. Four board CPU forwards pass the unchanged fixed and strict gates against both the original A FP32 references and the previous V-mean outputs. Worst relative L2 against original A is **5.1219e-8**, maximum error **7.1526e-7**, with zero strict outliers. These reassociated outputs are numerically equivalent under the gate, rather than byte-identical. One session, four finite saved outputs and complete cleanup are independently verified; heldouts remain excluded.

Fresh input-side SDK step1 then uses exactly the same four original calibration inputs and CONFIG, with staged paths as the only dataset change. It reuses no parent model/data/ranges and performs no extra explicit forward before SDK calibration. Worker time is **541.957 s**, including **539.174 s** in SDK step1, with **12,387.914 MiB** peak RSS. The measured pre-SDK reserve exceeds the unchanged 13 GiB guard; source, inputs, SDK release and the no-clock-write controller cleanup pass the saved audit. This is offline preparation memory. NPU compilation, actual conversion/fusion placement and runtime quality are separate pending checks in this record.

The **output-side** prototype instead moves restoration beyond the original output projection: `H(A + mu) W_O + b_O = H(A) W_O + [H(mu) W_O + b_O]`. The full 2,082-query projection stays, the original bias occurs once, and restoration feeds the unchanged LayerScale. It also adds one **1,048,576-MAC** mean product. Independent static review verifies channel order, broadcast shapes, all 146 untouched node protos and the complete inverse. This targets the observed restored-PV-to-INT8 projection boundary, but has **no board CPU gate, fresh calibration or NPU runtime result**. It is separate from both the input-side candidate and the old constant-bias fold. Neither candidate has accepted quantized accuracy or speed; changed grouping, actual precision, possible weight packing and branch launch cost still require measurement. Full-model, trajectory, realtime, sustained-rate, P95, stability and physical 8 GB acceptance remain outstanding.

Three SDPA operations and original learned updates remain INT8/NPU; the known internal FLOAT16 CPU transpose remains.

R2 keeps both raw initializers resident, binds two context-specific FD views of **one 40,648,704-byte pool**, and adopts each initializer exactly once into native IO. ## 234. Complete token-row suffix partition: byte parity and a measured component gain

The original global suffix is row-local across its complete 27-node computation. Splitting only its public token dimension from 2082 to 694 lets three copies execute concurrently on NPU core masks 1, 2 and 4. Every token, 1024-channel normalization, learned parameter and original FC2 product/add order is retained. All node and initializer protobufs are unchanged; restoring the five public dimensions reconstructs the original model byte-for-byte. The board CPU gate produced two byte-identical ordered concatenations before compilation. Actual compiler placement retained all internal operations on the NPU.

At 1 GHz, a matched A-B-B-A experiment on the same two captured cases measured the complete suffix call, including four original input finite scans/casts, DMA copies, all three concurrent runs and final FLOAT32 concatenation/finite validation. Mean latency fell from 210.116 ms to 135.466 ms (35.53%); both cases improved, and first calls were included. All candidate endpoints and repeated calls are byte-identical to the existing NPU suffix. Four resident runtime owners completed 16 SDK runs with balanced allocation and teardown; a separate query owner closed before execution. The controller restored its prior state.

This is a component result, not a full-model rate. Both routes retain the same conditional original-CPU relative L2 errors (0.005365 and 0.005455) and fail the unchanged fixed/strict tensor gates. The next integration must feed actual upstream native outputs, include the interpair handoff and measure the complete four-block path. Row-aligned FD offsets are only a possible follow-up: this run used readonly CPU FLOAT16 views and owned DMA buffers, with no imported subviews or shared scratch. Physical 8GB and trajectory accuracy remain unverified.

Evidence: [R169](../results/original_native518_suffix_token_rows_v169.json). Private model exports and captured arrays remain outside the public tree.


## 235. Row-partitioned suffix in a continuous four-block path

The three outputs are copied in order directly into the one owned pair1 input buffer; no intermediate reference arrays or FLOAT32 roundtrip enter this handoff.

Per-case reductions were 7.05% and 5.65%. All 112 logical stage hashes, four first endpoints, repeated calls and 48 row-input hashes match the existing native computation. ## 247. Disk relocation and actual tmpfs memory headroom are different results

[R182](../results/original_native518_archival_storage_and_shmem_v182.json) preserves all **198 historical files, full payload hashes and original paths** through two copy-and-symlink operations. Every destination is copied, fsynced and verified before any original pathname is atomically replaced; original directories and prior alias chains remain. Actual mount/device evidence corrects an initial assumption: **`/tmp` is root ext4, not tmpfs**. The first receipt's `released_tmpfs_allocated_bytes` label is therefore inaccurate: its 141-file move frees root-filesystem capacity while captured Shmem is unchanged. The original receipt is preserved with that correction. The first CPU launcher correctly rejects insufficient MemAvailable before graph staging or inference.

| Captured operation | Files / full payload bytes | Observed Shmem reduction | Destination free bytes at completion |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Root ext4 archives → data ext4 | 141 / 1,543,914,773 | 0 | 5,155,028,992 |
| Actual tmpfs staged sources → root ext4 | 57 / 1,432,249,184 | 1,432,084,480 | 658,722,816 |

The second operation raises measured **MemAvailable 2,931,544,064→4,362,502,144 bytes** and lowers **Shmem 13,145,923,584→11,713,839,104 bytes**, retaining the root 512 MiB floor and separate data 3 GiB floor. These system snapshots differ from the **1,432,412,160 allocated source bytes** and do not establish unique model-RAM savings or physical 8 GB feasibility. Independent audit checks the small captured inventories, all 198 hash/alias completion records, source/transport identities, mount classification and capacity arithmetic without reopening large payloads or accessing the board. Payload inode/device and original file-entry metadata change; **historical aliases and targets must remain immutable, with fresh paths for new writes**. Model math/weights, raw research records, drivers, mounts and reserved-block settings are unchanged. CPU gate results, inference speed, full-network quality and trajectory/realtime acceptance remain separate.

| Shmem | 11,738,316,800 | 10,478,383,104 | −1,259,933,696 |
| DMA-zone free | 1,007,341,568 | 1,874,067,456 | +866,725,888 |

Restoring the exact original host source later does **not** retroactively validate that transport. Independent saved audit verifies only the executed source/receipt/controller scope, **605 helper events, 80 memory samples, three residency views** and their arithmetic; it retains this integrity failure. No checkpoint content, model math, weights, mounts or drivers change, and no model is deserialized or executed. A later read-only inventory finds **all 2,795,115,035 bytes of ten original RKNN files resident**; no model-cache eviction is attempted in this record.
